## 2026-10-10 ✅ The opening recut B, as built: the arch opens as a place reward

Option B is built. The first day starts at noon (tick 0 is 12:00; one game hour is 50 seconds of play, and the clock stops while a menu or a dialogue is open), so dusk comes near minute six and the dark near minute seven. The beats:

- **The taught strike.** The bridge waits on one keystone in the shallows. One strike, with the minigame window, gives the first level and mends the bridge. The keystone regrows in ten seconds, so a miss gets a second try at once. Under the sign hangs a purse: 30 gold, the price of one tool in town, and the mushroom skewer recipe, so the reward is not coins alone (G4).
- **The find by the senses.** One dry log lies in the wet reeds below the bridge. It is a dig spot with no tool: it is picked up by hand, with no window and nothing worn. A find with no giver counts once the item has been in the bag, so the same quest can burn the log in the fire it builds.
- **The real choice.** Pell's stall says what each tool opens: the basket, Maru's bells in the garden; the trowel, his coin under the lantern. Whichever road comes first pays for the other tool: the bells pay 30 gold, and the coin comes with a basket.
- **The riddle at dusk.** From 19:00 to dawn, a prop that a riddle cites and that stands next to its answer lights it: the hill lantern glints over the tile to dig. By day the stones must be read.
- **The night discovery.** Handing in the coin, Maru says the broken arch shows a way after dark. A new quest with no giver asks for a visit to the arch's mouth at night; the stone beside it reads "By day the rubble, by night the way". Done, it opens the way under the arch, and the way leads to the threshold of the night ruins, where a bell rings once far below and nothing more happens yet.

**Why a place reward, not a path drawn only at night.** The decision of 9.10 left the choice to this build. A path that both clients draw only at night needs the 2D world scene and the 3D valley to draw a tile by the hour; both are outside this build, and the 3D client asked to leave the night path out for now. A path that the rules open and close by the hour also traps a player who stands on it at dawn, unless more rules move them. A place reward is one rule: a restore with no inputs, which never opens by hand, and a quest that opens it when it is done (`reward.opens`, schema-additive). Both clients already draw a restore before and after, and an open place stays open, like a mended bridge. The cost: after the first night the way stays open by day. The first visit still has to be made at night, and the threshold says so ("Not tonight. The way stays open.").

Along the way: the skewer recipe is declared uncommon, since a found recipe is one step rarer than its inputs (V-2); the kernel's version is 0.4.0, because the clock and the rules of a find changed.

**Waits for:** the axe and felling (CR-24) and the starters' kits (CR-37). The owner's answers of 10.10, written in parallel with this build, put the recut after them and change three of its beats: the first fire comes from a felled tree, and the explorer's find by the senses is its own axe hidden by the bridge, in place of the dry log; the real choice at minutes 4-6 is whom to help first, Maru or Pell, in place of the stall; and a first-tier recipe is known from the start (CR-61), so the purse under the sign holds something else that is new to do. The recut then lands with the one-time save reset (CR-62), which also carries the noon start. The rules built here stay: the noon start, a dig spot with no tool, a find with no giver counted once found, and the place reward that opens the arch. The threshold is the entrance of the night ruins, which is safe: the player only looks in.
**Closes:** the build of the 2026-10-09 entry on option B, once it lands.
**Retry when:** CR-24 and CR-37 are on `develop`; the 3D client draws the arch and the night ruins; or play shows the first dark too early or too late. Model: Claude Opus 5.5.

## 2026-10-10 ✅ The recut B on the axe and the starts: a fire from a felled tree, whom to help first, and one save reset

The question (10.10): the owner's answers of 10.10 put the recut after the axe (CR-24) and the three starts (CR-37), and changed three of its beats. The recut is reworked on top of both:

- **A fire from a felled tree.** The dry log, its spot in the wet reeds and the `core/wet-reeds` slot are gone. `core/first-fire`, now "A fire before dark", asks only for a campfire, built of 2 logs from an oak, with no meal, as option B has it. Every start holds an axe by then: the forager's kit, the digger's slid bank, and the explorer's glint by the bridge, which is now the find by the senses.
- **The purse holds gold only.** 30 gold under the sign, the price of one tool in town, and nothing else (the owner's word, 10.10). The mushroom skewer goes back to common and known from the start, so the previous entry's note on its rarity no longer applies.
- **Whom to help first, in place of the stall.** Arriving in town says it in two lines: Maru by his house and Pell at her stall could use a hand, and whom to help first is the player's. Both are open as soon as the town is reached. Maru's bells (`core/three-fruits`) pay with his old trowel. Pell's herbs (`core/herbs-for-pell`, which no longer wait for the bells) pay 30 gold and the herb stew recipe, the reward approved on 7.10. Both plants are picked with a basket, so a start that woke without one spends the purse on it at the stall, which still says what each tool opens. Maru's coin follows by the trowel's road and pays 20 gold and the herb tea recipe; then comes the night arch, as before.
- **One save reset (CR-62).** The save file is version 2. A save of an older version is dropped once, and the title screen says so once; a save that cannot be read is left as it was. The date a browser first played the demo is kept outside the save (`openfold.demo-since`, CR-85), written at the reset or the first new game and never cleared, so a later title for the demo's players can read it.
- **Schema, additive, format 1.** A reward item may name `instead`: what a start whose kit holds the item gets in its place (`rewardItem` in the kernel). The check refuses a reward item that a kit holds with no `instead`, and an `instead` that the same kit holds too. The kernel stays at 0.4.0, which this recut already brings.

Holes closed by default (the owner's word of 10.10 on small questions), each with the game it follows:

- **Each neighbour pays in what they have, and the first help is worth one tool either way.** Maru gives his old trowel; Pell gives a recipe and 30 gold, the price of a tool at her stall (was 15 gold). Like Willy in Stardew Valley, who hands the player his old bamboo pole.
- **No reward repeats a kit.** The digger, whose kit holds a trowel, gets a walking staff for Maru's bells. Like World of Warcraft's quest rewards, which since Legion fit the item to the character's class and specialization. The swap reads the kit, not the bag, so the check can prove it before anyone plays; a start that bought a tool may still get a second one, and keeps it, since no trader buys tools.
- **Neither help waits on a window.** The sunleaf opens from noon until dark (12:00 to 20:00, was 11:00 to 14:00), so Pell's herbs are open whichever help comes first, and so are Maru's bells (5:00 to 20:00). Like A Short Hike, where the island's helpers can be helped in any order. The headless walk proves it for every start and both orders (CR-86): each help's plant is open when the player gets there, with two game hours to spare for the walk.
- **Pell's herbs are an investigation.** They now follow the walk into town, which is a discovery, so they can no longer be one (G17): Pell's clue names the place, and the player reads the river to find the bank where the mist is thin. Like the contracts of The Witcher 3, the palette's example.
- **Maru's coin gives no basket.** Both helps are picked with a basket, so by the coin every start holds one and a basket would only repeat. The herb tea recipe moves from the bells to the coin, so neither of Maru's rewards is coins alone (G4).

The tier-1 recipes that the helps give, Pell's stew and Maru's tea, stay those quests' rewards: the owner answered CR-61 on 10.10 in the afternoon, and a recipe a quest gives is not among the tier-1 recipes known from the start.

The tests: the headless walk runs each start with each first help, from noon to the threshold of the night ruins. The browser walk: the phone's forager helps Maru first, gets his trowel, digs his coin at dusk, and is paid gold and the herb tea, with no second basket; the desktop's explorer helps Pell first, buys a basket with the purse and hands in the sunleaf.
**Closes:** the "Waits for" of the entry above, and GRUNT CR-62 with this recut.
**Retry when:** play shows that players do not see the choice in town, that one help is always taken first, or that the reset notice confuses. Model: claude-opus-5-5. Branch `feat/opening-recut-b`.

## 2026-10-10 ✅ The site leaves the pixel face and the shadow frame: a living window from the game is its one signature

Supersedes the site half of "One token sheet and the pixel frame tie the site to the game" (7.10); the game keeps Pixelify Sans inside its canvas and HUD. The product owner rejected the round-1 home page look (the structure and the builders board stayed approved): a pixel face scaled across a whole site reads as a costume, and the 2px frame with a hard 4px shadow on every card, button, chip and image is a stock kit where nothing stands out. Round 2 was judged on three phone stills that differed only in the type pair, plus one desktop still each and a clip of the motion.

- **Type:** display face Platypi for what is signed or counted (wordmark, headings, door names, builder handles, points); reading face Atkinson Hyperlegible Next for everything that explains or is pressed (body, nav, buttons, tabs, tags, ranks). Both OFL, self-hosted as pinned latin files with their OFL.txt beside them in `web/public/fonts/<family>/`. Rejected: a pixel face for the site (costume), a geometric grotesk (the template default), a serif body (editorial template).
- **One signature:** the classic door is a window into the valley, drawn from the core kit as CSS sprites at a whole-number scale (2 under 600px of window, 3 above), not a screenshot. On load it plays the opening's first quest once: the player walks to the broken bridge, mends it in four hammer beats, and a timber plate rises on the bridge-head post with the handle of the bridge record's author, the same handle the builders board lists beside it. Then only the valley idles (water, fire, the elder's blink, the oak, the lantern); the oak and the lantern rise one tile above the window's edge. No plate when the record has no author. The plate appears once more, unsigned, in the developers band. Nothing else on the page wears a frame except the two doors and outlined buttons; the hard offset shadow is retired (`--frame-shadow` goes from `design/tokens.css`).
- **Colour:** the nine-token subset stands; one accent, green, means go (Play, the developer area, the current nav item, the selected tab). Wood joins for the plate only, because a builder's timber sign is part of the art. Cream and gold stay out of the page chrome.
- **Motion, zero JavaScript:** every animation sits in `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`; the bare stylesheet is the finished frame (bridge mended, plate up, final numbers). Pixel motion steps one art pixel or one sheet frame at a time, never eased; text never moves. A 44px pause control (a checkbox and its label) stops the window and swaps the 3D loop for its still image (WCAG 2.2.2). The 3D door plays a 5.8s loop recorded frame-exact from the 3D trial (H.264 320x180, about 140KB), with `<source media>` so a reduced-motion reader downloads only the poster.
- **Animated counters, reversing PLATFORM 5.4:** the board's five totals count up once, tabular, no overshoot, the true number in the DOM for screen readers. Below 1024px, where the board is under the fold, each row tallies as it scrolls into view (a scroll-driven animation; without support the timed run applies); scrolling back above it re-runs the tally, accepted because a one-time latch needs a script.
- **Home criteria, restating PLATFORM 5.5:** criterion 1 becomes "at 390x844 the first screen shows the wordmark, the headline and lead, the classic door with Play, the 3D door with Enter, and the board's title and tabs"; criterion 4 becomes "under 300KB including the fonts, the scene sprites, the 3D loop and its poster" (measured 125KB without the 3D door and 281KB with it); criteria 2, 3 and 5 stand.
- **No sample numbers on a page:** until `pnpm hub:data` (GRUNT item 3) is part of the build, the boards on every page come from `content/board.json` and the creator profiles; the hub-data fixture reaches only the footer's build line, marked "(fixture)", and reviewers and this week show an empty state. Hub criterion 4 of PLATFORM 5.5 reads accordingly: the numbers rendered equal `content/board.json`'s.
- **Signatures keep the game's colours:** the boards page draws each builder's signature as the game does, in the pixel face (borrowed from `/play/fonts/`) with its own red, yellow, light moss and gold; those colours live in `web/src/styles/signature.css` only, not in the page chrome.

**Retry when:** players or the owner read the serif as a magazine rather than a game (then the one-family Recursive pair from the same round), or the bridge beat of the opening changes (the window follows the game's beat). Model: Claude Opus 5.5.

## 2026-10-10 ✅ A remark is said once, a topic waits for the world, and what was heard lives in the save

GRUNT S-1 and S-2 as built. A character's `remarks` wait for a `when` (quests done, on or not done, places mended, camp parts built, items held, slots visited, day or night); of those that hold and were not said, the highest `priority` wins, then the condition that names the most, then the first in the file, and it is said once in place of the standing line (salience, as in Valve's dynamic dialogue and Hades). A topic may wait for a `when` too; the menu shows four at most, the ones not yet asked first, so a character can hold eight topics and the check still refuses more than four that are always open (G21). The choosing is pure and lives in the kernel (`kernel/src/story.ts`) so the MCP server and the check can read it; what the player heard is presentation and lives in the save next to `met`, keyed by the remark's place in the file, so an edit that reorders remarks may let one be said twice, and never stops one being said. The two lines `storyBeats` held moved into the content (`talk.done` on the three fruits, `done` on the old bridge) and were cut to boxes of 80 characters, counted with a 16-letter name in place of `{name}` (G14); one box of the thanks had been 171 characters, out of the check's sight. The first remarks and the lanterns topic follow Maru's and Pell's voice sheets.
**Retry when:** creators reorder remarks often enough that repeats show (then a remark gets an `id`), or a player misses a remark that was said while they skipped the box (then a heard remark comes back once in a topic). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-10 ✅ The hook is the receding mist: a pocket for each starter, the bridge pushes it back, the name is carved at the bridge

The question (owner, 10.10): is the story strong enough to hold a new player, next to the big games and mobile games? Measured on develop: the first minutes are work (stones, a fire), the mystery of the bells is heard only at night, quietly and far off, and the player has no reason of their own to be there. Stardew Valley opens on a letter, Breath of the Wild on a view of the whole world, mobile games on a reward in the first minute; the story holds the player later. The owner approved four parts on 10.10:
1. **A mist pocket for each starter.** The three starters wake in three places (entry C1); each wakes in a small clear pocket inside the mist, and its first task (the basket, the dig, the senses) pushes the mist back a little.
2. **The bridge pushes the mist back.** When the bridge is mended, the mist visibly draws back from that part of the valley, the three pockets join into one valley, and a bell rings once, far off. Each place restored after it pushes the mist back further. Every belief reads it its own way (the Menders: what is tended remembers; the Wakers: something under it is waking; the Keepers: we are waking what should sleep).
3. **The name is carved at the bridge, not typed at character select.** The "Mended by" sign becomes the returners' stone: the camera glides down the names of those who came back before to the last empty line, and the player carves their own. Until then the player is "you". In stage 1 the names above are written content (the first ones who came back); with accounts in stage 2 they are the real players who came first, so a word filter is needed before then. Rejected: finding your own name already on a stone (the owner: strange); a character asking who you are (nobody stands at the waking place, and it would be a long first meeting).
4. **A waking line for each starter.** The idea is approved; the first examples were rejected as too indirect ("needs to be more direct"). The plain set (one object, one fact) waits for the owner.

The look, chosen from phone stills ([frame](docs/design/frames/2026-10-10-receding-mist.png), the waking pocket and the bridge after the mend): **B2**. The mist is clear in a circle of 5 tiles around the character and grows denser over the next 6 tiles to almost solid, 96% of the pixels at the edge of the map. It is near-black, not white: `#3a3554`, with `#5d6177` for the lighter patches and `#1b1a2e` for the shade. The gradient is made of dots (an ordered 4x4 dither on game pixels), because the style law allows only fully clear or fully solid pixels. The direction pad is the same dark colour and sinks into B2, so it gets a light outline. Not chosen: a circle of 3 tiles (A: the world too closed), a cloud-shaped grain (C: noisy on a phone), and white or light grey mist (the owner: grey-black). The criteria for the build: no mist inside the circle; at least 90% mist at the map border; from the character outward the mist only thickens; when the bridge is mended, the clear area reaches at least 7 tiles around it within 3 seconds and stays. The shade on a real phone is the owner's to judge on the preview.

The build touches the three starters (CR-37) and the opening recut (pull request #50); both are told before anyone builds. Today `start` takes and checks the name (1-16 letters, digits, spaces, `_` or `-`, in `kernel/src/step.ts`) and the title screen asks for it; carving the name at the bridge means `start` accepts an empty name and a later action sets it.
**Retry when:** players on a phone cannot find their way inside the pocket (then the circle grows before the colour lightens), the dither shimmers while walking (then the pattern is anchored to the world, not the screen), or the name stone needs a filter that is not ready when accounts arrive. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-10 ✅ Health and hazards (P-5a): a position report (`move`), the outing, and a fall that takes back what the outing brought

What was built, kernel only, no client and no content: a new action `move {at}` through which the client reports where the player stands. It is the only action that checks travel: the tile must be walkable and within 2 + floor(elapsed / `walk_step_ms`) tiles (Chebyshev) of the last report; every other action first runs the same arrival at the tile it names. This partly reverses "travel time comes later" (2026-10-07): the kernel still does not time a walk, it only refuses a report that could not have been walked. The health bar is still `player.satiety` (milli-points, lazy drain unchanged); the rename to hp waits until the crafting branches that inherit the old names have landed.
- **Outing.** The ledger begins on the first step onto unsafe ground and is cleared on a safe tile (a town tile, a slot with `safe: true`, or the first camp slot once any camp part is built). Kept gathers and digs are recorded in it; spending takes from the outing's gains first, so gains never exceed the bag.
- **Fall.** Only when something hurt in this step and health is at 0 (hunger alone never falls): the outing's gains are subtracted (no bag snapshot, so what was learned stays and nothing bought is lost), the quests solved and places visited on the outing are undone, and the player wakes in the last town square at full health. Event `fall` names what was lost.
- **Hazards** are map props with an id and a `hazard {kind, window, reach?}`: predator (a chase by the clock: first bite after `catch_ms`, then each `bite_ms`), pit, trap and deep water (once on entering). Only the `night` window exists; the mist waits for P-6. A hazard is escaped, never answered with an item (owner, 10.10). `pnpm check` refuses a hazard on safe ground (G30) and a predator whose worst run to safety costs a whole fall (G34).
- Evidence: 29 kernel tests (play unchanged on the shipped valley, bites independent of how often the client reports, replay determinism, fuzz) and 7 check tests. Measured: shipped content has no hazard, so a merge changes no play.
- Known wart (Codex review): a fall undoes the outing's riddle and visit markers but not a quest that finished by itself on the outing; its reward stays. Revisit if a giverless quest ever ends that way.
**Retry when:** a real phone shows walking blocked by a report refused for lag (then the allowance grows before the rule goes), or hazards need a second window before P-6. P-5a changes no play and can land when green; what must not ship without hazards is the later drain change. Model: claude-sonnet-5-5. PR #59.

## 2026-10-10 ✅ The owner's answers of 10.10 afternoon: lasting starter identities, phased progress, camp deals, weighted plot draws

The owner answered nine open questions on 10.10 in the afternoon, one at a time, after the integration pass found them unanswered in the docs or in sessions that stopped mid-question.
1. **The three starters differ for the whole game, not only the first minutes.** Each keeps a lasting edge (the forager finds more when gathering, the digger more in the ground, the explorer more rare finds) and abilities the others cannot reach; when weapons come, each plays a different style. CR-37 builds only the opening part; the lasting edge and the abilities need a design entry before any build.
2. **Multiplayer progress is phased, and the mist hides the gap.** A player who has not mended the bridge sees it broken and the far bank in mist; a friend who crossed walks into the mist and is gone from view. They meet once both have mended it. Shared building starts at stage 7.
3. **Warnings (G31):** count down to nightfall or to the mist's coming, whichever comes first; entering the mist, or the mist arriving, lights the lantern with one warning box, once per night; a burning camp warns before its fire goes out.
4. **A valley's day ends** when the player sleeps, or at 05:00 when the night is played through; the day summary waits until the player is in a safe place (like Stardew Valley).
5. **Any lit camp is safe, but a guest pays.** A camp's owner sets the price of a stay, even a simple one, and a stay is a deal the owner approves. This reverses the default proposed on 10.10 (a camp safe for its owner alone).
6. **Plot draws favour newcomers by odds, not tickets.** Everyone may sign up; the draw computes each player's chance in advance (higher for a player with no house or plot), and the sign-up shows that percentage.
7. **A recipe given by a quest stays that quest's reward** (CR-61): tier-1 recipes known from the start do not include it.
8. **The hub shows "Your card", levels and badges as "coming soon" boxes** until accounts exist, with no sample numbers.
9. **The 3D trial ships to production as a parallel version** behind its own button on the home page, on the same data, built alongside the 2D game; if the community prefers it, it gets the investment. The 64 px painted trial and the rich-pixel trial are archived with a tag.

Asked in the evening whether a fire's light keeps the hunting hazards off anyone in it, the owner chose: the safety itself is what a guest buys. Without a deal another player's camp is no safe place, at any stage, and the fire's light only marks how far the camp reaches. A day starts at 00:00 UTC, as the daily caps do, and a week at 00:00 UTC on Wednesday. Like RuneScape, whose daily reset is at 00:00 UTC and whose weekly reset is on Wednesday.

## 2026-10-11 ✅ Felling and digging run by themselves after one press, and the work is shown on the body and the tree
The owner asked that one press on a tree starts the felling: the character swings on its own and stops when the tree falls, with no pop-up and no bar. This is how RuneScape and phone farming games (Hay Day) do it, where Stardew Valley and Animal Crossing take one press per swing; one press suits a phone. Each swing plays an axe animation on the character, throws chips and a sound, and the tree shakes and cracks so the progress is read on the tree itself; moving away cancels the work, and the tree falls with its own animation. Digging and mining follow the same pattern with their own tool. The swing, dig and walk cycles are drawn with the character in the agreed pixel style and land with the walk animation.
**Retry when:** players lose track of how long a fell takes (then a small crack count on the tree), or a press by mistake starts work too often (then a hold to start). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-11 ✅ A tool's work runs on the kernel's own busy time, and a step away takes the action back
The felling of NEXT.md section 4a needs "moving away cancels", while the kernel applies a gather at once and refuses everything but a position report until `busyUntil`. Chosen: the press calls `act` with a fixed grade 3 (as the by-hand dig already does), the swings, chips and the tree's shaking run for the busy time the kernel returned, and a held direction other than the one toward the tree, or any window opening, calls `Session.cancelWork()`, which puts back the state and action log from before that action (`step` copies its state, so the old one is whole). Nothing was yielded, no xp or tool wear was taken and the node is ready again; once the busy time has passed there is nothing to take back. Scope: nodes that need a tool run by themselves (axe: oak trees and fallen logs; trowel: the dig spots; pick: a hook for a mining node that has no content yet). The stones and the keystone keep the strike minigame (the opening teaches it, GAME_RULES G13 and G15) and herbs keep their pick game. Walking was already a four-direction cycle (right is the left sheet mirrored); a step now shows its leg for 70% of the step instead of 50%.

## 2026-10-09 ✅ The crafting economy as built: no level wall, and a made good sells for more

The economy pull request builds two lines of "The crafting economy" above: the level wall goes (CR-17), and a made good sells for the made share (CR-18). It is stacked on wave 3, before that merges. Each number is a parameter that a vote can move.

- No level wall. The craft refuses only a missing station or one below the recipe's tier. Each level of the recipe's profession above the recipe's adds `craft_level_step_bp` (500) to the chance, and each missing level takes the same off, down to nothing and never below. At nothing, luck adds nothing: the try runs, misses, keeps the usual share of its inputs, counts as a try and trains the profession as much as any other. A tier-1 recipe is still sure at its own level and above. The journal and the craft panel no longer show the level a recipe wants, and they never showed a chance.
- Gear that fits the task, on the side of skill, is not built: no worn effect names a task yet, and `craft_chance_pct` stays on the side of luck. It waits as CR-32, next to the worn minus of CR-21.
- The made share. `k_craft_permille` is 750 (a vote moves it between 500 and 900, step 50). An item is a made good when its value comes from a recipe, that is, when the value table's cheapest source for it is a recipe; the bundle carries the sorted list. A recipe that makes a gathered item at a higher cost does not make it a made good. The trader pays `k_craft_permille` of r * V for a made good and `k_permille` (500) for the rest, rounded down. A tool still sells by the share of its uses left. The check refuses a made share below `k_permille`, and either share with a bound at 1000 or more, so no vote lets the trader pay what the shop charges.
- R8 and Theorem 3. R8 bounds each item's pay by its own share. Theorem 3 holds with the larger share, k_hi = max(k, k_craft), in place of k: wealth counts held items at k_hi, so making adds no more than the recipe's work, and selling a raw good at k only lowers it. The ceiling on gold per active hour rises from 3600 * k * r * P to 3600 * k_hi * r * P, by half at launch: the price of making pay more than selling raw (kernel review, section 8.6). The rules page's G40 and G41 say that a recipe's level is no wall.
- The values report prices each item at the trader's share for it. The new reading: gathering 7200.0 to 7920.0 gold an hour, tier 1 23850.0 to 27600.0 (median 25807.5), tier 2 11221.2 to 18360.0 (median 16848.0), tier 3 2115.0 to 3802.5 (median 2958.8). The share lifted tier 1 most, because its inputs are raw and the report counts what goes in at the trader's price for it. A whole chain from gathering to a made good stays under the ceiling above. Tier 3 at its own level and before luck still pays less than gathering. The made share does not answer CR-31; the levers are content and parameters, so CR-31 stays open with this reading.
- Kernel 0.5.0. The schema change is additive and FORMAT_VERSION stays 1. No value moves, and `values.lock.json` is unchanged.

**Closes:** CR-17 and CR-18 (crafting audit, Appendix D).
**Retry when:** players read a try at nothing as a bug, the market (CR-19) moves the trader's price inside R8's band, or a decision on CR-31 changes the tier-3 numbers. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

NEXT.md 4b, built inside G24-G27 and under the existing mute. The campfire's crackle, the stream's water and the dread of the ruins are three continuous beds on the ambience bus (so the master mute covers them) whose gain follows a pure falloff of the distance (`audio/spatial.ts`: 1 inside `near`, 0 beyond `far`, smooth and never rising; fire 1.5-12 tiles and only once built, stream 3-12 from the nearest water tile, dread 0-6 from the ruin tiles or an open predator's reach, never on safe ground, half as loud by day). `soundscape.ts` reads only the bundle, the state and a position, and the world hands the levels over every quarter second. Wave 4 of the crafting audit (section 6) is the client, inside the panels that exist, with no restyle and no new style rule. It is stacked on wave 3 and the economy pull request, before they merge. Since the economy, the station's tier is the one gate that blocks a recipe, and the screen shows no chance, no word for the difficulty and no wanted level.

The NEXT item 6 e2e ("the basket gone and the quest back at step one") contradicted the 10.10 decision that a fall takes back only what the outing brought, because the safe threshold closes the outing one step earlier. The e2e therefore proves a fall with nothing lost, and the loss stays proven in the kernel tests (agreed with the manager session).
**Retry when:** P-6 opens a way into the night ruins that does not pass the threshold (then the e2e can show a loss), a playtest shows the wolf's night reach over the day ruins catching players who never meant to go in, or the owner tunes `hp_low_pct` or the healing numbers. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-12 ✅ Night light: the player is cut out of the haze, a dark pool around a sharp lantern light

The owner (11.10, on v0.1.2): at night the mist was drawn over the character, so it blurred. The mist and the slot fogs are now one layer, and a WebGL stencil of the player's shape (a copy sprite that follows every frame, pose and crop) keeps that layer and the old picture's dark frame off the player; the mist still covers the ground, the props and the other figures. The dark frame left the camera filters and is drawn as a quarter-size canvas (`fx-frame`) with the same sine curve, under the cut. While the lantern burns, the cut runs on through the shade, the dark and the glow, and the player takes a warm tint instead: the one who holds the light is the clearest thing in it. Outside the light the night is multiplied by ink at 0.7 around a hole of 34 px with a two-step edge (`fx-dark`). Measured at 390x844 and 1440x900, day and night: player pixels differ by 0.0 from the frame without mist, fog and frame (before 10 to 57); ring outside the light 0.26 of the inside (before 0.60); edge 90% to 10% of the drop in 5 px at 390 wide (before 20).
**Tried and dropped:** a Mask filter on the haze layer. Any filter on a see-through layer came out brighter in Phaser 4.2.1 (the day frame came out 12 to 18 levels brighter per channel from the filters alone), so the layer would have changed its look. **Not covered:** the Canvas renderer keeps the old order (mist over the player, no frame). The cut-out also clears the mist over whatever stands in front of the player, a faint outline while the player walks behind a tree.

`/play3d/` is built by `game/vite.3d.config.ts` (`pnpm --filter game build:3d`, into `game/dist-3d`; its own public folder `game/public3d` holds the 3D models, so the classic `dist` does not grow by 30 MB). `game/src/play3d/world3d.ts` implements the same small `WorldView` the page UI asks of the Phaser scene (`app.ts`: bind, interact, beat, signText, screenOf) and feeds the same `Session`, so the kernel, the bundle, the save key and the save format are the classic game's by construction. Nodes, finds, people, camps and mended places are drawn from the bundle's instances (a Kenney model when `art.ts` names one, else the bundle's own sprite), so another creator's content shows in 3D from its first day. Proof (`game/e2e/play3d.ts`, chained into `pnpm e2e`): a character made at `/play/` loads at `/play3d/` (390x844 and 1440x900), walks a tile per press, gathers under the kernel, and `/play/` loads that save back. With `build:3d` forced to fail the classic build ships and the home page shows no 3D button.
Copied on purpose, not shared: the target/arrive/signText rules mirror `scenes/world.ts`, so the classic scene stays untouched while the 3D door is young. **Retry when** the two drift: move them into one pure module both worlds call (a shared-file change, its own small pull request). Left on `trial/3d` for now: the cell trial (`cell.html`, Poly Haven assets) and the old free-camera valley page. The 3D camera has no rotation yet. Model: sonnet-5-5. Branch `feat/play3d`.

## 2026-10-13 ✅ The frames of stealth, tending and the first expedition, and how the three build on each other

The product owner's word (13.10): build the next three steps of the variety order (the entry of 9.10) now, stealth over the hazards, then tending, then the expedition, each in two pull requests (the kernel, then the client with the content), with the frames below set by the design session as defaults, to be confirmed or changed by the owner at the merge. What the kernel holds today, read for this: a hazard is a still prop with a reach, a chase ends only at a safe place or at dawn, no fire has fuel, no quest step reads time, there is no sleep, the clock runs only while the world is on screen, and the content kinds are a closed list.

- **Stealth is a watched road.** A predator may carry a round: a closed loop of tiles it walks after dark at `pace_ms` a tile, its place at any tick read from the clock alone (no new state, so a replay agrees); by day it sleeps on the first tile of the round, where its sprite stands. It sees a cone `eyes` tiles long ahead of it and half as wide as it is long, and it smells anything one tile away; a prop marked `cover` (reeds, tall grass) hides a player standing on it from the cone, never from the smell. Seen means the chase the hazards already run (bites by the clock until a safe place or dawn, G34), so the way out stays the run back, and a predator without a round keeps today's reach. The first quest is the south bank: a wolf rounds the meadow west of the river after dark, a reed thicket along the water is the cover, and Pell wants a reed that opens its head only after dark at the bank's south end. Being seen costs the run back over the bridge to the camp, as in Untitled Goose Game, where being caught costs a retreat; the check keeps that run under a fall (G34).
- **A fire is kept by hand.** A map prop may be a fire: it holds `fire.cap` logs and burns one in `fire.log_ms` on the game hour, so it burns only while the player plays (away, nothing happens to it, SETTLEMENT 3.4), and feeding it by hand (one wood item from the bag, standing beside it) lights it or adds a log up to the cap. A `tend` step holds when the fire burned without going out from one dusk to the dawn after it. A burning fire warns `fire.warn_min` game minutes before it goes out (CR-94). A prop may read by a fire (`lit_by`): its words show only while that fire burns. The first quest is Maru's hearth: light it before dark and keep it to dawn, with wood from felling, as in Stardew Valley, where a thing kept alive asks for a return at its hour. The camp's own fire burns no wood yet: its safety still reads "a part stands" until the tent (CR-25) puts this fire on the camp at `wood_per_night` times `d`, and CR-88 then reads safety from it. Nothing sleeps in the kernel today, so a night is kept awake at the screen; when sleep comes it burns the night's wood at once (the entry on the world and its clocks) and so ends a vigil.
- **The expedition is the night ruins, in two pull requests.** The kind `expedition` (`place`, `window` with its bells, `exit`, `chain`, `climax`, `reward`, `on_miss: wait`) and its rules come first with no content: inside the place after dark the bells ring on the hours the content names; at the last bell the mist walks the player to the exit with no damage and the outing closes there; a quest of the chain begins only inside the place inside the window and keeps its progress across nights; the climax is standing on its slot with the chain done before the last bell, once, and it applies the reward, a place that opens by day. Then the content: the ruins' yard watched by the wolf's round and a glowcap that opens after dark in the hall (stealth), a night dig in the deep ruins with one rare find in five (gamble), the hall's brazier kept to dawn, fed before the last bell with enough to reach the morning (tending), and the hall's inscription read two ways, a Waker's and a Mender's (riddle, STORY.md S2 and S4), with a visit at night as its answer. At the climax the stair under the hall opens (the last bell only walks out), and its cellar is a dig spot by day whose find hints at a fold. Four types, none twice (G17); the doorstep's pit and the wolf are the hazards among them (G36); the three-stones riddle and the arch stay the opening's (the entrance entry, 10.10).

Why this order: the expedition is made of the first two, the round and the fire, so each lands small and proven before the container needs it. Rules G47-G49; the specs are NEXT.md sections 12-14; the stealth, fire and expedition rows join GAME_RULES section 8.
**Retry when:** the first playtest shows the wolf's window too wide or too narrow (tune `pace_ms` and `eyes`, never the rule), a night kept at the screen reads as a chore (then the fire's cap and `log_ms`, or how long a night is), or the expedition's players stop at a beat the journal shows. Model: Claude Fable 5.1.

## 2026-10-14 ❌ A full fire took a whole log for a tick of burn

Found by the review of the tending client (NEXT 13b): the merged feed (NEXT 13a, #104) added `log_ms` to a burning fire clamped at `tick + cap * log_ms` and refused only exactly at the cap, so a press one tick after the cap took a whole log for one tick of burn, with no refusal. The journal asked for logs right then, and the client's e2e passed only because it kept its own copy of the cap rule. Fixed in the kernel: a log goes in whole or not at all, and a fire with less than a log's room under its cap refuses it with "The fire is already full." while the log stays in the bag. `nextLogAt` gives the tick from which the next log fits, and the client says it; `burnsAsWood` and `tendHolds` are the kernel's own wood and vigil tests, exported so the client keeps no copy. Whole logs tie the fire's numbers together: a cap of one would take a log only once the fire is out, which relights it, so `fire.cap` has a minimum of 2, and `pnpm check` refuses fire params whose warning opens before a log fits (`warn_min` over `cap - 1` logs of burn). Covered in `kernel/test/fire.test.ts` and `tools/test/fire.test.ts`. `KERNEL_VERSION` stays 0.7.0: no release carries it yet. The G48 clause follows pillar 5 (effort is never wasted) and is the owner's to confirm at the merge, as with the frames of 2026-10-13.
**Retry when:** players press Feed at a full fire again and again (then the button can wait for `nextLogAt`), or the owner tunes the cap, `log_ms` or `warn_min` (the check keeps the three consistent). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-15 ✅ The home page carries the Web Analytics script, its only script

Criterion 2 of PLATFORM.md 5.5 kept the home page free of scripts. Without the analytics script on it, a visitor who lands on the home page and leaves is never counted, so the site cannot say how many people came, where from, or how many of them went on to play. The script is Vercel's own, deferred, about 1KB and sets no cookie; the home page now carries it and nothing else, and the two home tests allow exactly that pair.
**Retry when:** the home page's first paint or its transfer budget (criterion 4) suffers, or the project drops Vercel Web Analytics. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-15 ✅ The hourly refresh reads the counters with Redis's read-only token

GRUNT item 14 compares the hub-data the hourly loop run builds with the deployed one and deploys production only when they differ. Production's document carries the usage counters (`usage`, `sources.usage`) because the Vercel build has the Upstash variables; the Action had none, so its document had `usage: null` and every run would have read `changed` and deployed every hour. Two ways out were weighed: (a) give the Action read access to the counters, or (b) leave `usage` out of the comparison and deploy anyway when production's counters are older than six hours. Chosen: (a). `usage.ts` also accepts `KV_REST_API_READ_ONLY_TOKEN`, and the repository holds two more secrets, `KV_REST_API_URL` and that token, which can read and never write. The Action then builds the same `usage` as production, so a new visit reaches the creator pages within an hour and a quiet hour deploys nothing; (b) would have left the counters up to six hours behind and still deployed four times a day for nothing. Measured before the change: production's document and one built from the same `main` commit differed only in times, `usage` and the weekly summary posted between the two builds. `same.ts` leaves out `built_at`, `sources.*.fetched_at` and `usage.since` / `usage.until` (the window moves with the clock). A run reads 84 Redis commands with today's 68 records, about 60,000 a month, an eighth of the free plan's 500,000. Without the two secrets the step warns and compares nothing; without the hook it is skipped.
Also fixed: the six-hour schedule (`23 */6 * * *`) no longer matched the loop step's check for `23 * * * *`, so every scheduled run posted the weekly summary instead of running `loop all`. The schedule is hourly again; the repository is public, so Actions minutes are free.
**Retry when:** the counters move to stage 2a's own store (then the Action needs that store's read access instead), the free plan's command budget gets tight, or a refresh waits too long behind a credits-only commit (below). Model: claude-opus-5-5.
Two review findings shaped the workflow. The refresh is its own job with its own concurrency group (`hub-refresh`): in the shared `loop` group a pending run is replaced by the next queued one, so a burst of pull request events could have starved it. And Vercel runs the ignored build step (`vercel.json`, `ignoreCommand`) for hook builds too, so while the newest commit on `main` changes only `CREDITS.md` a hook build would be cancelled; the job makes the same check and, instead of firing a hook that cannot build, warns that the next commit to `main` carries the refresh.

## 2026-10-16 ✅ The kernel tells a hunt begun, a hunt outrun and a kept night lost to a fire gone out, and an outrun hunt gives back what it bit

The client could not tell when a hunt began or ended, or when a kept fire went out, without keeping its own copy of the kernel's rules, and Pell said "no bite marks" to a player the wolf had bitten. The kernel now tells three things and records one (`KERNEL_VERSION` 0.9.0, the kernel half; the cues come with the client). `seen` (the predator's id) is told once, at the step where a predator notices the player and the hunt begins, by its reach or by its round's eyes (G47), and never again while that hunt goes on. Ground that takes the last of the health is a fall (G33) and no hunt begins over it, so nothing is told seen as the player falls. `safe` (the predator's id, and `bitten` when the hunt bit at least once) is told when a hunt ends because the player reached a safe place (G30), walked to or made safe by a camp part built under their feet; a hunt that ends at dawn, in a fall, at an expedition's last bell (G49) or with its predator gone is not told. Nor is a safe tile reported after the last bell: the player stood inside at that bell, so the mist ends the hunt there, with nothing told and nothing given. At `safe` the hunt gives back what its bites took, up to `hazard.predator.refill_bp` of the cap, 7500 basis points in the core (bounds 5000 to 10000, step 500): health becomes the larger of itself and the smaller of that share and itself plus the bites. It never lowers health, a hunt that never bit gives nothing, and health lost before the hunt stays food's to give back (G32). The first build lifted health to three quarters at every `safe`, and review found that farmable: at 30% health, a step off the doorstep into the wolf's reach and back gave 75%, as often as the player liked, which a refill meant to soften a hunt outrun was never for. The refill came with the game-feel wave the owner accepted on 2026-10-14; the bound is the reading that keeps it from healing what no hunt took, and the G32 clause that says so is the owner's to confirm at the merge.
`fire_out` (the fire's id) is told when a fire that a `tend` step of an open quest keeps goes out in the night it was keeping, so that night's vigil is lost (G48; DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-13: a step holds when its fire burns without going out from a dusk to the dawn after it). With D the last dusk at or before its `until`, the out is told when the fire was lit by D and went out before that night's dawn (`lit <= D` and `until < nightEnds(D)`). An out at or after dawn ends a night already kept, and an out by day or before dusk loses no night (a fire relit before dusk keeps it again), so neither is told; nor is the out of a fire lit after dusk, or relit after its night was lost, because that night was lost before it. It is told once, at the first step at or after its `until`, like a bell, but before the action's own events. It needs no state: a step tells the kept fires whose `until` lies after the tick of the state it starts from and at or before its own, and each `until` falls in exactly one such gap, so a burn is told once, a fire fed in time is told only if its longer burn ends in the night, a refused step tells nothing and the next accepted one does, and a replay cut at any step tells the same (R4). A fire no open quest keeps is not told, because no rule reads its going out today: G48's warning comes before the out and is the client's, and `lit_by` reads only whether a fire burns. G30 and G31 will need it once a camp's safety reads its fire (CR-25, CR-88).
`player.bitten` lists the quests that were open when a predator bit the player on an outing that came home, sorted and kept. A bite marks the quests open when it lands on the outing's own list (the optional `outing.bitten`); they join `player.bitten` when the outing ends on safe ground or at a walk-out, and a fall drops them with the rest of the outing (G33), so no giver speaks of a bite the player fell to. A bite with no outing to carry it (a hunt kept from a state with none) is recorded at once. Any predator's bite marks every quest open at the time: the giver sees bite marks, not which predator made them. The optional `talk.done_bitten` (one to three lines, counted by G14 like `done`; the assembler refuses it without `done`) is the giver's line at the hand-in of a marked quest, and `doneLines(bundle, state, quest)` picks it, else `done`. The south bank keeps its `done` ("no bite marks") for a run unseen or outrun unbitten, and gains Pell's `done_bitten`. All of it is additive: the schema gains `refill_bp` and `done_bitten`, the state gains `player.bitten` and `outing.bitten`, `FORMAT_VERSION` stays 1, and a 0.8.0 save loads with no bites recorded (`step` and `Session.restore` fill `bitten`) and is never told of a fire that went out before its last step. The client draws nothing for the three events yet and still shows `done`; the cue pull request switches the hand-in line to `doneLines`. Tests: `kernel/test/events.test.ts` (each event when stated and never otherwise, the bound on what comes back and nothing gained by stepping in and out of reach, a lost night told once and no out that loses none, the record a fall drops and the one a fall keeps, a replay cut at every step, a 0.8.0 save), with the hazards, expedition, stealth, session, play and assembler tests brought along. Rejected: a refill to three quarters at every `safe` (farmable, above); telling every out of a kept fire, or every out at night (`isNight(until)`), which also tells a fire lit after dusk and a relight after its night was lost; a stored count per fire like the expedition's `rung` (the clock gap tells each burn once, with nothing to migrate); health before the hunt stored on the chase (its bites already count what it took); a refill at the last bell's walk-out (G49 walks the player out and gives nothing back); a third line for seen but not bitten (`done` already fits it).
**Retry when:** a playtest finds the refill too kind or too harsh (tune `refill_bp` within its bounds, never the rule), the owner reads G32 the other way at the merge (then the plain floor comes back with a guard against the doorstep farm), a camp's safety starts to read its fire (then its out is told too), a second predator guards a quest's way (then a bite may mark only the quests whose way it guards), or a second quest wants a line for another outcome of its run. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-16 ✅ The vigil closes at dawn: a tend step is met only once its night is over

A `tend` step was met as soon as its fire's wood reached dawn: lit by the last dusk, with `until` at or past that night's dawn. Three logs ahead of the clock carry the hearth from 23:00 to dawn, so from 23:00 the tracker said "Bring it to Elder Maru" and Maru said "It held, the whole night" six game hours before morning, in the dark. The owner's word (14.10): the vigil closes only at dawn. The words must be true, and every word already promises a night kept to its end: the journal's "Keep the elder hearth burning until dawn", Maru's "Feed it until dawn" and "It held, the whole night", the frames of 2026-10-13 ("from one dusk to the dawn after it") and the schema's "from dusk through dawn". So the rule moves, not the words.
The rule: a `tend` step is met only at or after its night's dawn (05:00), with the fire lit no later than that night's dusk and burning without a gap to dawn. Before dawn it is not met, however much wood the fire holds; after dawn it stays met through the day until the next dusk, as before, even when the fire is lit again by day (below). `tendHolds` adds `tick >= nightEnds(lastDusk(tick))`, and `questSatisfied`, the hand-in and the client all read it, so the hand-in comes only after 05:00 and a tend quest with no giver finishes itself at the first step at or after dawn. While the fire has wood to dawn and dawn has not come, the journal line says "it has the wood: wait for dawn" instead of ticking the step.
A relight by day keeps the night. A fire lit at 19:00 and fed as the journal asks (three logs at 19:00, one at 21:00, one at 23:00) has wood to 05:00 exactly, so the hand-in now opens on the very tick it goes out, beside a hearth that offers Feed whenever the bag holds wood. A log fed then lights a new burn with `lit` after dusk, and on its own that undoes the night just kept: review found the vigil unmet again at 05:01 and at noon after one such log. So when `feed` lights a fire that is out while its vigil holds, the new burn carries that night's dawn as `kept`, and `tendHolds` holds from that dawn until the next dusk for a fire whose `kept` names it. A top-up keeps `kept`, a second burn the same day carries it again, and a fire lit after the next dusk, or after a night it did not keep, carries none. `kept` is optional and additive (the canonical form leaves it out when absent), `FORMAT_VERSION` stays 1, and a 0.9.0 save loads as it is: its fires have no `kept`, a vigil it showed as kept before dawn waits for dawn, and a quest already handed in stays done.
The warning follows the journal. On that night the burn reaches its last `fire.warn_min` at 04:30, while the journal says "wait for dawn", and the box (G31) told the player to feed a fire that loses nothing by going out at 05:00. The box now opens only while the fire still needs wood for the night it keeps (by day, the coming one): its vigil does not hold, it burns, it was lit by that dusk and its wood falls short of that dawn, which is exactly when the journal counts logs (`wantsWood` in `game/src/fires.ts`). A burn by day that will not reach the next dawn still warns; a burn with wood to dawn, one lit after dusk, and a burn by day while a kept night's vigil holds do not.
`fire_out` follows the kernel-events entry above: an out at or after the dawn of a kept night is not told, so that night, whose wood ends at 05:00, ends untold, and its hand-in tells no out. `KERNEL_VERSION` 0.10.0: no release carries a tend quest yet (v0.1.4 has none), but develop has carried one since #120 under 0.8.0 and 0.9.0 keeps the old rule, so a release cut before this change would take a hand-in at 23:00; the version names the rule a save or a replay ran on (R5).
On the planned night ruins (NEXT 14b) the hall's brazier is fed before the last bell at 4 and its step is met at the dawn after the walk-out, so the quest after it in the chain and the climax come on a later night. That changes the frames of 2026-10-13 for the night ruins, and like them it is the owner's to confirm at the merge. A tend quest with no giver finishes at the first step the kernel hears from dawn to the next dusk, and the client sends a step only when the player acts or reaches a new tile: a player who stands idle from dawn to dusk leaves it open, and that dusk loses the night. No such quest ships today (the long night has a giver); before the brazier lands, either the client reports a step at dawn while a tend step waits for it, or the kernel credits a dawn that passed between two steps.
GAME_RULES G48 says it. Tests: `kernel/test/fire.test.ts` (not met at 23:00 with wood to dawn, nor at 04:59 with more; met at 05:00 and handed in only from then, with no out told; the journal's night ends untold; a gap at 04:00 fails, relit or not; met until the next dusk; a relight at 05:00, a tick later or at 06:00 keeps the night and the giver takes it, a top-up keeps it, a second burn carries it again, and a fire lit after the next dusk or after a night not kept carries none; a replay agrees, cut at every step), `kernel/test/expedition.test.ts` (a brazier kept through the night inside the place is met at the first step from dawn, outside it, and the next quest of the chain waits for the next window), `game/test/fires.test.ts` (the journal line and the giver wait for dawn; the warning opens only while the fire needs wood for its night, exactly when the journal counts logs) and `game/test/session.test.ts` (a 0.9.0 save restored at 23:00 in the middle of a vigil waits for dawn, and the giver takes it at 05:00). The tending e2e waits for "it has the wood: wait for dawn" where it waited for the hand-in at 00:40, and fails if the hand-in shows before dawn.
Rejected: keep paying once the wood reaches dawn and change the words instead ("Fed to dawn - tell Maru" in the tracker, Maru's "It will hold till morning"). Wood to dawn cannot fail, so it spares up to 300 s at the screen, but every promise of a night kept would turn into a promise about wood, and "It held, the whole night" would still be said in the dark. Also rejected: a relight by day that drops the kept night, with texts that say so and a client that warns before such a Feed (fed as the journal asks from 19:00, the fire goes out on the tick the hand-in opens, so many players would meet it, and the words "stays met until the next dusk" would carry a condition); keeping the 04:30 box with a second line that asks for no wood (a warning about an out that loses nothing).
**Retry when:** a playtest finds the wait between the last log and dawn reads as dead time (then shorten the night or tune the logs, never pay before dawn), a quest with no giver gets a tend step (then the dawn report or the kernel's credit above comes first), or sleep lands: it burns the night's wood at once (the entry on the world and its clocks), and a night slept through needs its own answer on whether it was kept. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-16 ✅ The night ruins get one way in, open by day, and the doorstep's pit becomes the night's toll

The owner accepted the experience audit's fixes for NEXT 14b on 14.10, and the review of the expedition container (#119) added two notes; NEXT section 14 now carries them, before 14b starts. The way in: the doorstep's south tile (36,22) turns from rubble to ruin floor and walls close the rest of the ruins, so the threshold and its doorstep are the place's one way in, open at every hour once the arch is open, and the hall's brazier can be lit before dusk. This replaces the seal at (36,22) of P-5c (2026-10-11); the rubble at (35,21) and (37,21) stays. The doorstep's pit then lies on the only road, so at night each crossing, in or out, costs its 30% of the bar: the toll is meant, the pit stays where the floor is broken (G34), and G34's run counts it with the bites. The garden, the lantern spot and the meadow path to the tangle's mouth force the place to about x 32 to 39 and y 21 to 31, so the gate rubble falls inside, the deep ruins stay a day dig outside, and the gamble's pocket is new, inside the walls. The brazier is told by the world (the cold brazier and its Feed button, the inscription too dark to read) and by one topic of Maru's, a Mender's hint with no bell, hour or count (her sheet forbids a straight answer about the bells), instead of an anchor on the tend step. Under the vigil rule of the same day (the vigil closes at dawn) the brazier's step is met at the dawn after the walk-out, so the riddle and the climax come on a later night, and the inscription needs the fire again then. The glowcap's hours run [20, 4); the bell pit allows at least 8 digs a window, pinned by a dig loop, and its other rows turn up something on most digs (G15). The bells get a sound of their own, a chip with the time to the last bell, and a report of the standing tile when a bell rings, since the kernel hears only actions and reports. The walk-out lands the work under way instead of dropping it as a fall does. The climax gets a sound, a banner and lines of its own (`climax.lines`) and reward items, so it speaks and pays the same whether the stair was opened by the bells or shored by hand. The threshold's first-visit box stays as the opening's invitation.
One question goes to the owner at the merge, with a default: a treasure has no hours, so once the deep way is open the bell pit can be dug by day, and a find with no giver is met by the journal, so a shard dug at noon finishes the gamble the moment it begins at night. The default accepts it: the quest still begins only inside the place inside its window, the deep way opens only through the chain, and the window's cadence is the night player's pace, not the gamble's pressure.
Rejected: an anchor on the tend step (lit by the visit's second bell), since the accepted fix keeps the brazier a plan with no kernel change; a second doorstep tile at (37,21) and (37,22), a route past the pit that would turn the toll into a detour; an optional `hours` on a treasure, read by `dig` as `gather` reads a resource's, a kernel change this plan does not assume.
**Retry when:** a playtest shows players miss the brazier's rule (then the anchor), the toll reads as a punishment rather than a price (then the second doorstep tile, still one way in), or the owner keeps the gamble to the night (then the treasure's `hours`, an additive kernel pull request before 14b). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-16 ✅ The tracker, the chips and the toasts at 16px, two toasts at a time and low on a phone; the tracked task keeps its rule

G29 sets text at 16px or more; the quest tracker and the HUD chips were at 14px and the toasts at 15px. All three are at 16px now. The tracker's cap is 23em instead of 320px, as wide in letters as before, so a step breaks where it did; on a phone it takes up to 368px of a 390px screen. On a phone (420px wide or less) the chips take a row of their own under the health meter and the buttons, across the whole width: one row where under the meter alone they took two or three. The tracker under them stands a row or two higher, and that keeps it off the player where the camera stops at the top of the map: at Maru's hearth the long night's step takes three lines, and the last of them covered the player's head. The health meter and the phone's HUD buttons stay at 14px; they were not among the three.

At most two toasts are on screen, a fading one counted. A third waits its turn and is never dropped: a toast is often the only word for what a press did (a refusal says when a log fits, a pickup what came into the bag). While one waits, the oldest toast up leaves once it has been read, 40ms a letter, 1.2s at the least and never later than its 2.8s, one fade (0.5s) at a time. A bad line (a bite, a refusal, a miss) goes ahead of the calm ones, and the oldest calm toast up makes way for it at once, so it is up within half a second. When one action closes a task and opens the next, the two lines share one toast, one line each, the closed task first; a task that opens and closes in the same step is told once, as done. A fall clears the toasts and the line, since its box says it all. The rules live in `game/src/toasts.ts`, apart from the page and on a clock the page passes in, and `game/test/toasts.test.ts` covers them, with the first campfire's real events among the cases.

On a phone the toasts sit at the bottom, just above the action prompt. At the top of the map the camera stops, so the player and Maru's hearth stand high on the screen, where the toasts used to cover them; low, they cover neither. At the foot of the map the camera stops too and the player stands low, so when the player's feet pass 60% of the screen height the toasts go up under the tracker, and back down once the feet are above 55%, so a step to and fro does not toss them. Low on a phone they sit where the dialogue box opens, so a box holds them: from the moment it opens nothing new goes up and the clocks of those already up stand still, and the held ones go up when the last box of the beat closes, after a mend's story box for example. A desktop keeps the toasts at the top, beside a box, and never holds them. On a phone 664px tall two toasts end just under the player's feet. During Pell's outing on the south bank they cover a strip a few tiles south of the player instead of a few tiles north.

The tracker still follows its rule (the tasks that need nobody first, then by id), not the newest task. The order of `player.quests` cannot tell which task is newest: the keys keep the order the tasks opened in only until a save, which sorts them (R3), and `game/test/tracker.test.ts` pins that. Two ways would make it exact. The client could keep a list of the order the tasks opened in: no kernel change, but lost on a reload and outside a replay and the state hash. Or the kernel could record `player.questsAt`, the tick each quest turned active, set wherever a quest opens (`accept`, the quests that open by themselves and the chains), `{}` in an older save, kept by a save and a replay: exact and deterministic, an additive field that needs a new `KERNEL_VERSION`. The tracker would then show the highest tick and break a tie by today's rule.

`game/e2e/hud.ts` (in `pnpm e2e`, before `play3d.ts`) proves it in a browser at 390x844 and at 1440x900: the mend's toasts wait for its story box on a phone and stand beside it on a desktop, two at a time and none dropped, the two task lines in one toast; a bite goes up ahead of a pickup's calm lines; every toast goes up clear of the player and of the action prompt (on a phone, below the player's feet); and on a phone the chips take the whole width under the meter and the buttons.
**Retry when:** a playtest misses a toast (the line moves too slowly, or a box held it too long), a phone about 640px tall puts two toasts on the player, a toast hides the bank wolf's approach, or the kernel records when a task opened (then the tracker shows the newest). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-16 ✅ The bank wolf shows only its eyes, red and steady; a hunt is seen, heard and drawn, and outrun

The owner picked still 3 of `docs/design/frames/2026-10-16-wolf-eyes.md` (the frame is `docs/design/frames/2026-10-16-wolf-eyes-3-glow.png`): the bank wolf's eyes are two squares of 2x2 game px, 2 px apart, in #ff1744, in a soft glow of whole-pixel rings 1, 2 and 3 px out (the red added as light at 0.4, 0.2 and 0.08). They are steady at full strength while the wolf is awake: the pulse is gone and there is no blink. At night the wolf's body stays in the dark; only the eyes show. The red is off the 32-colour core palette, an exception the owner made for these eyes alone to the style compass of 2026-10-09 (light that stays on the palette): no other light, eye pair or prop takes it. It is written once, in `WOLF_EYES` in `game/src/predator.ts`, and `game/test/stealth-cues.test.ts` fails when it turns up anywhere else in the game's code, content or design sources.

The moment the kernel tells `seen`, the predator growls (made by the synthesizer, no recording), the walking wolf's eyes flare (a glow two rings wider and brighter in every ring, at full strength for 0.3s and back to the steady glow by 0.9s), a "!" stands over the predator for 1.2s, the camera gives a short jolt (left out when the player asked for reduced motion), and one bad toast goes up, "Seen! Run for a safe place", in the voice of the bite's line and ahead of the calm ones by the band's rule. The music turns to a hunt's own song at once (a quarter of a second) and back over 2.5s when the hunt ends, however it ends: safe ground, a fall, dawn or the predator gone. The song follows the kernel's `player.chase`, not the events, so every end turns it back.

While the kernel's hunt names the bank wolf, its sprite holds for 0.4s where it saw the player, then runs after them at its pace (600ms a tile) over the ground the player may walk that is not safe, so round the water and over the bridge once it is mended, and waits beside them. When the hunt ends it walks back at twice its pace and takes up its round where the round has got to. It is drawn only: the kernel keeps the predator on its round, sees from there and bites by its own clock, and the client keeps no copy of a rule. The scene draws the wolf from `wolfDrawn` in `game/src/predator.ts` every frame, and the tests drive the same function through the kernel's own hunt.

`safe` plays one sound, two soft notes going up, and a short good toast, "Safe."; the health the kernel gives back shows in the meter. At the hand-in the giver's line is the kernel's `doneLines`, so Pell has the line for bite marks when a predator bit the player on the south bank and the outing came home.

Not in this change: a narrower window for the moonreed crossing, a darker tuft on the cover, and a round one tile off the cover.
**Retry when:** a playtest misses the flare or the "!" (too short, or too small at a phone's zoom), the growl startles more than it warns, players read the drawn wolf beside them as the one that sees and bites (the kernel's wolf is still on its round), a second walking predator needs eyes (it does not take this red), or the 3D door draws the hunt. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-17 ✅ Beyond the mist: one column of smoke per region, the world's edge a lip, and a border's mist may cut into the clear circle

The owner's pick of D1 in the open-world draft (`docs/design/OPEN_WORLD.md`, section 4), made on the three phone stills of 2026-10-16 (`docs/design/frames/2026-10-16-landmarks.md`, #129): both 2 and 3, and yes. A region shows one landmark above the mist, a column of smoke in single puffs that bends a little at its top (still 2), drawn at its true place inside the view and at the view's edge in its direction outside it. The grid's outer edge, the fog that never lifts, is a lip of earth and stone one tile wide with the mist pouring off it and no ground under it (still 3); a fog band that will lift keeps B2's look (still 1), so the two never look alike (G54). Near a border the band's mist cuts into B2's clear circle and the border stays dark up to the player, as in all three stills: there B2's first criterion (no mist within 5 tiles) gives way to its second (at least 90% at the border). Why: a phone shows only 1.1 to 3.6 tiles past the circle, so without a landmark nothing behind a band is ever seen and a long walk has no goal the eye can hold; and an edge drawn as fog would promise a place that never comes. Nothing is built by this pick: the landmark layer (about 80 lines) and the edge's look belong to the draft's stage 5, after B2's edge, and a river border wider than 4 tiles may now be weighed, since its far bank can be seen by its landmark.
What the stills cannot show, for the build to measure on a real phone: the smoke's drift over B2's dots may shimmer in motion; at night the column dims with the world (a faint glow at its foot is the fallback, not yet drawn); the landmark held at the view's edge slides as the player walks and may read as a compass or a HUD marker.
**Retry when:** a walk-by on a phone shows the smoke shimmering or the edge marker reading as a HUD arrow (then redraw it, and drop it only with the owner), or a region has nothing that would make smoke (then that region's landmark goes to the owner). Model: claude-opus-5-5.
Later the same day the owner made the world endless (the next entry): the lip of still 3 has no edge to stand on and waits for one, and the edge of the part opened so far keeps still 1's look, since it will lift. The smoke and the yes stand.

## 2026-10-17 ✅ The world has no end: generated ground around the hand-made valley, grown by the community, and a first look that says the world is huge

The owner's word (17.10), answering the open-world draft (`docs/design/OPEN_WORLD.md`): the world has no size limit, and not every way on needs a crossing. As a start the map opens so there is somewhere to walk; resources are scattered at random and the ground is generated, and places are added as development goes (a lake, a cave, a town). One developer cannot match twenty years of building, so the community designs and grows the world; but from the first session a player must feel "this world is huge", even while it is unfinished, and each release adds a layer.
The shape, recommended and accepted the same day: the valley stays hand-made at the centre and the opening never moves (G59). Around it lies ground generated from one seed, the same for every player and every load, integer-only in the kernel. Danger rises with distance from town (G64) instead of doors, and a crossing stands only where the story wants one. Authored places, the core's and the creators', are stamped onto the generated ground at chosen spots and stay. The world opens ring by ring: the first ring about 3 x 3 chunks (192 x 192 tiles, about 15 times the valley), measured on a phone before the next. Pillar 1 stays a ceiling, with the chance inside it: the gaps between pays are random and shorter on average, and what pays varies (a find, a ruin, a view, a far smoke), but no stretch passes 90 seconds; an average alone would allow a five-minute empty walk that no average shows. Closest model: Valheim (one seed, biomes harder with distance from the centre, hand-made locations stamped in). Kept from RuneScape, whose whole map is drawn by hand: places built by hand, and danger by distance (the Wilderness).
What it changes: in the draft, G50's 1024-tile cap, the grid's edge (G54) and crossings as the only way on (G55, G58) give way to the above, and a generator, chunk streaming and a save that keeps only what changed take the place of a stitcher of region files; G62 and G64 stand. D2 is answered in direction: creators grow the world, in a frame set when that stage comes. The spec's stage 1 is one town and one area (G-1), on purpose to keep the complexity in hand, so the open world is built after v0.1.5 and the night ruins, and starts as two or three phone stills of the generated ground around the valley (the direction rule).
**Retry when:** the first ring on a phone reads as empty or samey (then more authored places per ring before a bigger ring), the generator cannot keep pillar 1's ceiling without filler that fails G8, or a phone cannot draw the paged ground within its frame budget. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-17 ✅ The order from here: the opening's hook, v0.1.5, accounts and a server beside the open world, then players who see each other without chat, chat last

The owner's word (17.10): what interests him most now is multiplayer and the open world, and most important of all is that the first quests go through the renewal, with logic and a hook. The order, recommended by the manager session and accepted the same day:
1. **The opening's renewal.** Logic and a hook in the first quests, building the hook approved on 2026-10-10 (the receding mist: a pocket for each starter, the bridge that pushes the mist back, the name carved at the bridge, a waking line), which never got a NEXT section and is not built, and planting the endless world and the other players in the first session. A review and a proposal come first (`docs/design/OPENING_REVIEW.md`), then phone stills for the looks, then the build.
2. **v0.1.5**, the release of what develop holds.
3. **Accounts and a server** (the spec's stage 2a), beside **the open world** (the entry above: stills, then the first ring).
4. **Players see each other walk in the world in real time, without chat.** The spec puts live presence and chat together at stage 7, the last before creator code (section 16); this pulls presence forward and keeps chat, which needs heavy moderation for players of 13 and up, for last.
5. **Chat.**
The rest of the spec's roadmap is not reordered by this entry. Light presence (others' camps, tracks and notes, stage 4 with the market) may come before or after step 4; that is set when step 3 closes.
**Retry when:** names shown to other players (the returners' stone, a seen player's name) prove to need chat-level moderation (then step 4 waits for it), the server's running cost is out of reach for step 4, or a playtest of the renewed opening shows the hook still does not hold (then the opening goes on before step 3). Model: claude-opus-5-5.
